# Agent Capacity Completion and Production-Readiness Plan

**Status:** Local agent and capacity production-readiness is complete; supervised project development may begin
**Last updated:** 2026-07-23
**Production readiness:** Ready for the local supervised-development transition; hosted deployment remains intentionally blocked
**Formal sequential completion:** 15 of 15 phases complete for the supported local runtime. The withdrawn synthetic-provider result has been replaced by configured real-Codex evidence.
**Estimated backend readiness through phase 12:** Complete for the local runtime.
**Closed phases:** Phases 0 through 14 are complete for local operation. CAP-024 remains an explicit hosted-only blocker and does not prevent local project-agent development.
**Hosted acceptance:** Blocked by the intentional hosted-deployment suspension

This document is the controlling plan and completion ledger for the capacity-provider governance, allocation, provider runtime, AgentKernel, agent content, engineering starter, and research starter architecture. Every phase begins by rereading this document, the workspace `AGENTS.md`, and the linked canonical architecture documents. Every phase ends by updating the evidence and remaining-work sections here.

No phase may be called complete because a type, route, mock, fixture, or source-string assertion exists. Completion requires the owning implementation, removal of conflicting paths, executable tests, and service-workflow evidence.

## Execution Order And Status Discipline

The numbered phases in this document are the sole production-completion sequence. The older five-phase architecture roadmap is historical implementation context and must use `Architecture Milestone` terminology; its numbers are not completion-plan phase numbers.

1. Work proceeds in ascending completion-plan phase order. A downstream live workflow may be used to test an earlier phase, but it does not advance the downstream phase while an earlier prerequisite remains open.
2. A newly discovered defect is assigned to the earliest phase that owns the broken invariant. It reopens that phase when it contradicts the phase exit gate.
3. Only the Current-State Audit Register, Implementation Phases table, and Remaining Closure Ledger state current status. Evidence entries are historical observations and must explicitly identify superseded intermediate conclusions.
4. Percentage estimates are forbidden until every phase has a finite, binary checklist and the percentage is generated from satisfied checklist items. Manually assigned phase percentages are not completion evidence.
5. Historical phase closures remain evidence records, not current status. CAP-252 through CAP-298 reopened owning phases after the no-provider-double audit and real-provider reruns exposed invalid evidence and production defects. Only the Implementation Phases table and Remaining Closure Ledger below state current phase status.

## Mission

Deliver one production architecture that:

- gives each team one regeneratable broadcast registration key;
- gives each provider installation one global Ed25519 identity that can join multiple teams;
- lets teams approve or reject provider membership without implicitly granting capacity;
- issues independently revocable provider/team credentials after approval;
- enforces explicit grants, versioned allocations, reservations, provider-native limits, and provider-local limits;
- performs duration- and budget-bounded planning and properly governed acting;
- autonomously performs real engineering and cited research from the first-party starters;
- exposes every backend capability through typed API, CLI, and configuration surfaces without depending on Admin UI;
- accesses and mutates agent content only through TreeDX;
- makes every execution forensically traceable and every settlement exactly once;
- contains no duplicate, legacy, placeholder, compatibility, or unused capacity/agent implementation.

## Nonnegotiable Boundaries

| Owner | Canonical responsibility |
| --- | --- |
| `@treeseed/sdk` | Versioned portable contracts, schemas, pure admission/allocation helpers, provider-neutral security/config primitives, reconciliation contracts |
| `@treeseed/api` | Registration governance, memberships, credentials, availability, grants, allocations, workdays, demand, assignment function, reservations, leases, usage, settlement, audit, TreeDX proxy authorization |
| `@treeseed/agent` | Provider manager, multi-team local coordinator, runner, execution-provider adapters, AgentKernel, tool mediation, provider-local enforcement and recovery |
| `@treeseed/cli` | Complete operator/provider command surface over public contracts; never a scheduler or policy owner |
| Engineering/research starters | Project-owned agent definitions, prompts, activity profiles, permissions, output contracts, and work semantics; no custom copies of built-in runtime handlers |
| TreeDX | Product-neutral repository/content storage and query; no Treeseed capacity semantics |

Additional rules:

1. One admission evaluator, assignment function, reservation path, settlement path, provider protocol client, human control-plane client, kernel execution entrypoint, and artifact owner.
2. A replacement becomes canonical only when the conflicting implementation, export, route, scope, schema, test, and documentation are removed in the same phase.
3. Production modules target 150-350 lines and must remain below 500 lines unless this file records a reviewed exception. Oversized `app.ts`, `store.ts`, SDK barrels, CLI handlers, runner, and kernel files must become composition points.
4. No capacity or agent production module may use `@ts-nocheck`, `@ts-ignore`, `@ts-expect-error`, relaxed compiler settings, or disabled lint rules.
5. Only `plan` and live execution modes exist. There is no dry-run implementation or terminology.
6. Providers initiate outbound control-plane contact. The API never depends on inbound provider connectivity.
7. Infrastructure lifecycle flows through reconciliation. Memberships, grants, allocations, sessions, assignments, leases, and ledger entries remain API records.
8. TreeDX is the only production agent content access/mutation path. Filesystem access is limited to code worktrees, non-content evidence/reports, and explicit fixtures.
9. Admin UI work is out of scope. Every future UI action must already have an API and CLI operation; declarative policy must also have a configuration representation.
10. Hosted Railway/Cloudflare deployment remains suspended. Nothing in this work may recreate push deployment or mutate those providers outside the reviewed reconciliation path.

## Target Trust Model

### Global provider identity

Each provider installation generates one Ed25519 keypair locally. The private key is held in encrypted Treeseed configuration or a host secret manager. The API stores the canonical public JWK, its SHA-256 fingerprint, an identity version, and status. Provider ID is derived from the fingerprint, so the same identity is recognized across teams.

Provider proof is an EdDSA JWS over method, canonical path, SHA-256 request-body digest, audience, issued/expiry times, unique `jti`, fingerprint, and identity version. Default validity is five minutes, clock skew is 60 seconds, and each `jti` may be consumed once. Every mutation also requires an idempotency key.

### Team registration key

Every team has one current key generation with a high-entropy plaintext, safe prefix, verification hash, separately encrypted reveal value, status, and audit metadata. Only an authorized team manager may reveal, rotate, enable, or disable it.

Rotation immediately invalidates the previous generation and atomically cancels all pending requests created with that generation. Approved memberships and credentials are unaffected. Keys and credentials never appear in normal logs, lists, traces, assignment records, or reports.

### Membership and credentials

Registration creates a pending provider/team request. Approval creates only an approved membership and credential-issuance authorization. It creates no grant, allocation, reservation, or work authorization.

The provider uses signed proof to exchange the approved request for a membership-scoped opaque credential, revealed once and stored only as a hash. That credential mints 15-minute access tokens, refreshed with five minutes remaining. Credential revocation invalidates its access tokens immediately. Team and provider can rotate credentials independently from provider identity and the broadcast key.

## Target Durable Model

The clean schema contains:

- `team_capacity_registration_keys`
- `capacity_providers` as global identities without team ownership
- `capacity_provider_registration_requests`
- `capacity_provider_team_memberships`
- `capacity_provider_team_credentials`
- `capacity_provider_access_tokens`
- `capacity_provider_proof_nonces`
- `capacity_provider_availability_sessions`
- provider manifest execution-provider definitions plus membership-scoped availability snapshots
- membership-scoped capacity grants
- immutable/versioned capacity allocation sets
- capacity workdays, demand records, and participation cycles
- transactional capacity reservations
- provider assignments and mode runs
- idempotent usage actuals and append-only ledger entries
- capacity audit events

The target schema requires explicit status constraints, referential foreign keys, timestamps, stable unique business keys, and indexes for team/provider/membership/status/lease/workday access. The generated clean baseline and service workflow now prove this ownership graph, including rejection of nonexistent teams and orphan registration/membership records; CAP-033 is resolved. Since the system has not launched, existing capacity data and schema lineage are reset rather than migrated or dual-read.

## Allocation and Admission Contract

`CapacityAllocationSetV2` is strict and versioned. It contains reserve policy, project portfolio slices, class slices, mode slices, explicit borrowing rules, effective interval, version, and status. Every slice has `targetPercent`, `minPercent`, `maxPercent`, and `hardCapPercent` with:

```text
0 <= min <= target <= max <= hard cap <= 100
```

Sibling targets sum to exactly 100 after reserve. Active sets are immutable and can only be superseded. Borrowing defaults to denied; overflow is explicitly `deny`, `approval-required`, or `borrow`. Zero means denied, null means unspecified, and an unlimited grant requires explicit `unmetered` policy.

The sole evaluator is SDK-owned:

```ts
evaluateCapacityAdmission(input: CapacityAdmissionInput): CapacityAdmissionDecision
```

It combines membership, live availability, grant, workday, allocation hierarchy, reservations, settled usage, provider-native facts, local offer constraints, mode/class/capabilities, and acting provenance. It returns an allow/deny result, stable reason codes, maximum reservable amount, selected grant/allocation version, calculation explanation, policy snapshot, and required approval.

The API loads and locks relevant state, evaluates, inserts the reservation and assignment, advances demand/participation state, and commits as one transaction. Concurrent requests may not exceed a hard cap.

## Canonical API Surface

### Team governance

- `GET /v1/teams/:teamId/capacity-registration-key`
- `GET /v1/teams/:teamId/capacity-registration-key/reveal`
- `POST /v1/teams/:teamId/capacity-registration-key/rotate`
- `POST /v1/teams/:teamId/capacity-registration-key/enable`
- `POST /v1/teams/:teamId/capacity-registration-key/disable`
- list/show/approve/reject/cancel team provider registration requests
- list/show/suspend/resume/revoke provider memberships
- list/rotate/revoke membership credential metadata
- `GET /v1/teams/:teamId/capacity-audit-events` with provider/membership/action/resource filters and keyset continuation

### Public/provider onboarding

- `POST /v1/provider-registrations`
- `GET /v1/provider-registrations/:requestId`
- `POST /v1/provider-registrations/:requestId/credential`
- `POST /v1/provider/access-tokens`

Registration uses `Authorization: Treeseed-Registration <key>` and signed provider proof. The approval response never exposes a provider credential to the team reviewer.

### Provider runtime

- create, refresh, and close membership-scoped availability sessions
- poll next assignment
- accept/renew/return/complete/fail assignment
- start/complete/fail mode run
- report usage actuals

The old register/heartbeat/check-in/live-registration overlap is removed without aliases.

### Governance/runtime records

Typed list/show/validate/plan/apply or lifecycle endpoints cover grants, allocations, project agent classes, workdays, assignments, reservations, usage, ledger, execution traces, and audit export. Lists use stable pagination, team scoping, filters, and deterministic ordering.

- `trsd capacity registration-key show|reveal|rotate|enable|disable`
- `trsd capacity registration-requests list|show|approve|reject|cancel`
- `trsd capacity providers list|show|suspend|resume|revoke`

- reconciliation-backed `build|up|status|logs|down|test-local`

All commands support `--json`. Mutations are idempotent. Declarative changes expose `plan` and live `apply`. Secrets are read through protected stdin or secret references and are emitted only by explicit reveal/rotation commands.

## Provider Manifest Version 2

The provider owns one identity, provider-global execution providers/native limits, and multiple connections:

```yaml
schemaVersion: 2
identity:
  privateKeyRef: data://identity.json
executionProviders:
  - id: codex-primary
    adapter: codex
    nativeLimits:
      maxConcurrentRunners: 4
connections:
  - id: team-a
    marketProfile: staging
    # Optional when transport differs from the canonical signed-proof audience.
    marketAudience: https://api.example.com
    teamId: team-a
    providerId: provider-shared
    membershipId: membership-team-a
    membershipCredentialRef: secret://capacity/team-a
    membershipCredentialId: credential-team-a
    offer:
      weight: 2
      maxConcurrentRunners: 3
      capabilities: [engineering, research]
```

No plaintext secret is allowed. The broadcast registration key is accepted only as one-time `provider join` input and is never persisted in the manifest. A new manifest may contain `connections: []`; signed status polling and one-time credential exchange materialize the complete approved connection and its secret reference before provider runtime reconciliation can consume it. Connection offers narrow but never widen provider-native capability. Explicit percentage shares total no more than 100; weights default to equal. One manager supervises every connection and a shared runner pool.

The provider-local coordinator uses weighted-deficit round-robin, reserves a provider-global slot before polling/accepting, isolates every connection, and reconciles active leases on restart. Provider-local/native hard limits always win.

## Agent, Content, and Starter Target

`AgentKernel.runAssignment` is the only production entrypoint. The API decides eligibility and mode; the kernel performs preflight, loads context, resolves the assigned activity profile, filters tools, dispatches one execution, validates one artifact manifest, records telemetry, and classifies failure. Legacy queue and cycle APIs are deleted.

Tool-mediated TreeDX operations are the sole owner of content artifacts. Handlers validate tool receipts and return artifact references; they never write a second deterministic note. A structured artifact manifest links assignment, mode run, agent/class/handler/activity, tool events, TreeDX content refs, source worktree/commit, verification, citations, signals, usage, diagnostics, and errors.

Engineering retains architect, engineer, releaser, reporter, researcher, reviewer, technical writer, and tester. Its acting graph is test-first: approved decision and ready node, exact-ref worktree, research/architecture as needed, failing test, integration ref, implementation, verification, review/revision, documentation, release readiness, and operations-runner integration. Agents never directly push, merge, tag, deploy, or mutate provider infrastructure.

Research retains researcher, reviewer, technical writer, and reporter. It decomposes questions, defines source criteria, searches/fetches through a provider-neutral controlled adapter, writes linked evidence notes, synthesizes, validates citations, revises unsupported claims, and publishes a cited knowledge artifact and deterministic report. Citation records include URL, title, optional author/publisher/published time, retrieval time, content hash, bounded excerpt/license, claim IDs, and confidence.

Starter TypeScript handler shims are deleted. Project specialization lives in content-defined activity profiles, prompts, tool policy, permissions, and output contracts.

Each phase must add focused unit/integration coverage, update affected guarantees, run package-local verification, update canonical docs, remove replaced paths, and record evidence below. A phase with unresolved required items remains `in progress` or `blocked`.

## Canonical Sequential Exit Contract

These are the controlling exit criteria from the supplied implementation plan. They are cumulative and sequential: phase N cannot close while any earlier phase is open.

| Phase | Binary exit criteria |
| --- | --- |
| 0 | Every known old and new path appears in the inventory; every issue has one selected owner and target phase; no architecture ambiguity remains; no implementation begins before Phase 0 closes |
| 1 | Fresh installs contain only the target schema; public contracts compile independently; capacity code is typed and modular; no legacy data compatibility remains |
| 2 | One provider identity has approved memberships in at least two teams; neither team can observe or mutate the other membership; all legacy registration paths are deleted |
| 3 | One provider process safely serves multiple teams; provider-global budget cannot be oversubscribed; per-team failures remain isolated |
| 4 | Every assignment has active membership, grant, allocation version, and reservation; stored policy demonstrably changes admission; no duplicate allocation math exists |
| 5 | Real workdays are not fixed-count smoke tests; planning and acting use the same assignment path; the live-workday reservation inconsistency is eliminated |
| 6 | Concurrent retries cannot create duplicate usage or settlement; every terminal assignment has one explainable financial result |
| 7 | No production caller can reach a legacy kernel/manager path; no duplicate kernel validation or mode decision remains; public exports contain only target runtime |
| 8 | Exactly one output owner exists; no production content path bypasses TreeDX; starters require no custom TypeScript handlers |
| 9 | Engineering starter autonomously plans, implements, tests, reviews, documents, and reports a real scoped change; no dummy chain or project handler code remains |
| 10 | Research starter autonomously performs research, evidence collection, synthesis, review, revision, knowledge publication, and reporting |
| 11 | No governance/operational action requires Admin UI; CLI and API expose the complete backend system with configuration for declarative policy |
| 12 | Active guarantees execute actual lifecycle behavior; no active guarantee relies only on mocks/source strings/file presence; local workflows repeat and leave no orphaned state |
| 13 | Local/package environments are operationally supportable; security/recovery/performance targets are evidenced; hosted readiness remains explicitly blocked while suspension remains |
| 14 | Final audit traces every required workflow, finds no duplicate/legacy/unused/compatibility/direct-content/untyped path, verifies packages/interfaces/guarantees/docs/cleanup, and publishes an honest ready/incomplete/blocked report |

The detailed tasks, tests, required guarantee families, failure cases, verification matrix, and definition of 100% in the supplied canonical directive remain mandatory even when a row above is concise. Phase evidence cannot waive them.

## Required Guarantee Families

- registration key uniqueness, reveal audit, rotation cancellation, disable/enable;
- signed registration, proof expiry/replay/rate limits;
- approval-is-membership-only, rejection, suspension, revocation;
- credential exchange/rotation/revocation and access-token invalidation;
- multi-team isolation and provider-global no-double-spend;
- grant requirement and allocation target/min/max/hard-cap/borrow enforcement;
- atomic idempotent reservation and exactly-once settlement;
- fair agent participation and planning continuation;
- acting decision/readiness/capacity provenance;
- TreeDX-only content and one artifact owner;
- complete execution trace;
- autonomous test-first engineering;
- autonomous multi-source cited research;
- crash, replay, retry, lease-expiry, and revocation recovery;
- full CLI/API parity.

Active guarantees must exercise the real API, database, provider manager, runner, kernel, TreeDX path, and settlement lifecycle. Mock, file-presence, source-string, or call-order evidence is insufficient.

## Verification and Edge Cases

Required verification includes package-local SDK/API/Agent/CLI builds and tests, starter standalone validation, integrated fixture verification, focused guarantee validation/runs, `trsd capacity test-local`, local reconciliation acceptance, forbidden-legacy scans, compiler-suppression checks, import-boundary checks, source-size checks, and duplication checks.

Concurrency/security scenarios include key rotation racing registration, proof replay/forgery, approve/reject races, multi-team simultaneous final-slot requests, revocation during lease, token refresh failure, manager restart, API outage during renewal, allocation supersession with active reservations, hard-cap concurrency, duplicate completion/usage, partial settlement, TreeDX loss after mutation receipt, unsupported research claims, and idempotent CLI retry.

Hosted provider acceptance and cleanup are required only after the hosted deployment suspension is formally lifted. Until then, the final readiness report must preserve CAP-024 as a blocker.

### Phase 2 audit, implementation, and exit report

The opening re-audit found CAP-103 through CAP-109; retained historical claims did not waive them. All seven findings are resolved. Credential and access-token plaintext are deterministically re-materialized only for an exact idempotent issuance response while only prefix/hash/metadata remain durable. Every governance mutation now binds its scoped idempotency key and request digest to a durable result or CAS winner. Registration-key rotation returns one generation/key for an exact retry and one audit event. Approval/rejection has one transactional winner and cannot leave an orphan membership; revoked provider/team membership is terminal. Proof nonce cleanup/classification is transactional and storage failures propagate. Durable registration throttles replace the process-local map across team/IP/fingerprint/key-generation, and pending expiry is materialized before read/review.

Identity rotation requires old- and new-key proofs over one canonical body, adjacent identity versions, atomic nonce consumption, and one immutable rotation record. Team credential rotation creates a generation-based issuance authorization, revokes prior credentials/tokens, and never reveals the successor secret to the reviewer; only the signed provider exchange obtains it. Provider-initiated rotation enforces its narrow scope. Membership inspection/read authorization and cross-team denial are explicit. Registration admission locks the active key generation and atomically creates identity/request records, so rotation either follows and cancels the request or wins and leaves neither a pending old-generation request nor an orphan identity.

**Phase 2 exit report (2026-07-18): complete.** SDK proof tests pass 4/4 and the standalone SDK build passes. API governance/static tests pass 17/17; API route-descriptor tests pass 11/11; the standalone API build passes. The real-PostgreSQL proof passes 4/4 and covers the clean 11-table canonical selection, reversible-credential/legacy absence, transaction rollback, 21-way limiter concurrency with exactly 20 admissions, concurrent dual-proof identity rotation preserving two memberships, and registration-key rotation racing request admission. ### Phase 3 active audit and implementation log

The opening audit reread the controlling plan and all five canonical capacity documents, inspected the manifest/config/identity/coordinator/manager/runner/client/session/state paths, focused runtime tests and planned guarantees, runtime documentation, package scripts, and recent capacity history. CAP-110 through CAP-114 invalidate the retained focused-service completion claim. The current weighted-deficit class correctly prevents duplicate in-process generic slots and produces the expected 3:1 sequence in isolation, while the coordinator independently joins two teams, persists separate credentials, refreshes separate access tokens, and isolates a missing secret. Those useful primitives are not yet the required durable multi-process coordinator.

The canonical live topology runs manager and runner roles separately, but availability-session sequence, runner pressure, active slots, and completed diagnostics lived only in module-level maps; the runner role—not the provider manager—owned weighted polling and slot dispatch. Runtime concurrency summed execution-provider maxima instead of applying the stricter host-wide ceiling, had no atomic execution-provider/lane/native-budget claim, and published full discovered capabilities plus zero active pressure to every team. Credential/token responses were not checked against the configured team/provider/membership binding. Existing tests were mock/focused tests and cannot close restart, API-outage, final-slot, lane-budget, or service-workflow guarantees. Phase 3 remains active until these findings are resolved and the exact checklist passes.

The first implementation pass adds a locked, atomic, mode-0600 provider-local capacity state shared across processes. Global, per-connection, execution-provider, lane, and reserved-credit claims survive store/process recreation, admit exactly one simultaneous final slot, bind assignment/lease identity immediately after polling, feed manager pressure without exposing lease secrets, and are released only after the runner lifecycle returns. A losing execution-provider/lane claim returns the just-leased assignment with `provider_local_capacity_exhausted`. Availability-session id/sequence and short-lived access tokens are durable, so recreated manager/coordinator instances refresh the exact session and continue using a still-valid membership token during a temporary token-endpoint outage. Host-wide concurrency is the strict upper bound over summed execution-provider maxima. Availability capabilities and lane capabilities are projected to the connection offer, and access tokens must match the configured membership/credential binding. Manifest validation rejects duplicate team/membership connections and mixed provider identities. The duplicate process-local `runRunnerSkeleton` live/background scheduler and its active/completed maps were deleted; a pure plan renderer remains. A package-owned static gate enforces the Phase 3 module size, typing, suppression, and retired-name boundaries.

The manager/runner ownership correction on 2026-07-18 resolves CAP-110, CAP-111, CAP-113, and CAP-115. The provider manager now owns weighted team selection, `/assignments/next`, and atomic persistence of admitted leases as durable `ready` dispatches. Runner processes only claim `ready -> running` work and execute the supplied envelope; the fallback runner poll path is deleted. Ready dispatches survive restart, while manager startup observes and safely returns uncertain `running` leases; control-plane failure retains the claim. One team's revoked availability publication is isolated before scheduling, so healthy memberships continue without cross-team polling or trace leakage. Team projections narrow provider/lane concurrency as well as capabilities, and status snapshots omit the full dispatch envelope. Access-token issuance carries required team/provider identity and the coordinator requires exact team/provider/membership/credential binding; mixed-provider manifests and cross-wired tokens fail closed. The same atomic claim accounts for arbitrary reservation-native units from provider budget limits and reserve buffers, in addition to global/connection/provider/lane concurrency and credits. Local lifecycle failures retain running work for recovery; confirmed terminal/return and lease-expiry outcomes create bounded secret-free durable evidence.

**Phase 3 exit report (2026-07-18): complete.** CAP-110 through CAP-115 are resolved. The package runtime suite passes 30/30 across ten files, including a real socket-level two-team service workflow with 3:1 weighted polling, separate membership tokens/sessions, manager-created durable dispatch, state/manager recreation, no runner repoll, real HTTP token-refresh outage/recovery, running-lease observation/return, isolated revocation, and cleanup. Locked state tests prove concurrent final-slot exclusion plus connection, execution-provider, lane, credit, and arbitrary native-unit limits. SDK provider/governance tests pass 12/12; API governance/access tests pass 16/16; Agent package-shape tests pass 6/6; SDK, API, Agent, and CLI standalone builds pass. Static scans find one assignment-poll invocation owned by the manager, no process-local retired runtime, no legacy host credential mode, no compiler suppression in the focused Phase 3 modules, and no focused Phase 3 module over 500 lines. The three binary Phase 3 checklist items are satisfied. Phase 4 subsequently closed; Phase 5 is current. The oversized runner remains explicitly assigned to Phase 7 under CAP-022.

### Phase 4 audit, implementation, and exit report

The opening audit reread the controlling plan and canonical allocation/admission sections, traced SDK grant/allocation contracts and the sole evaluator through API repositories, services, state loading, admission counters, atomic reservation/assignment persistence, CLI/client consumers, guarantees, schema constraints, focused tests, and recent ownership documentation. CAP-116 through CAP-120 replaced the stale focused-service completion claim and are now resolved.

The completed implementation has one parent-relative hierarchy evaluator and one atomic admission path. Minimum and target positions produce deterministic priority signals; normal reservations stop at maximum; overflow stops at hard cap and requires configured reserve or sibling borrowing; donor minimum/target capacity, prior borrowing, approval state, and reserve exhaustion are authoritative. Database claims separate recipient normal, overflow, reserve, donor, and rule commitments, so the transaction enforces the exact pure decision without recalculating policy. Grant creation is planned-only and activation revalidates membership, project, provider inventory, lane ownership, capabilities, expiry, and compare-and-swap state. Allocation validation and activation prove durable references, immutable effective intervals, and overlapping-only supersession. **Phase 6 opening audit (2026-07-18): active and incomplete.** The audit reread this controlling plan and the five canonical capacity documents, then traced the clean baseline, SDK financial contracts, reservation/counter claims, runtime settlement route, assignment lifecycle, membership/workday terminalization, usage and ledger readers, provider-local lease recovery, focused tests, and recent history. The current 468-line settlement module contains two write implementations: single settlement and copied zero-usage batch release. Single settlement performs two pre-insert replay reads, claims a mutable reservation token, uses unconstrained `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, and polls after the batch instead of relying directly on the existing unique settlement-key and reservation/phase constraints. Usage identity is only `usage:<assignmentId>` rather than assignment attempt plus usage dimension/idempotency key. Failure settlement and lifecycle CAS are separate recoverable operations without a durable failure classification. Overrun handling uses `INSERT OR IGNORE` and has no approval/rejection operation. API lease selection can reclaim an expired lease but does not durably classify or reconcile orphaned attempts. CAP-128 through CAP-133 own these findings. Phase 6 implementation must replace these paths in place and may not add another financial writer.

**Phase 6 implementation checkpoint (2026-07-18): active and incomplete.** The clean baseline and SDK usage contract now require a report idempotency key, nonnegative durable assignment attempt, and stable usage dimension, with unique idempotency and assignment/attempt/dimension business keys. Settlement derives and validates attempt provenance from the durable assignment, rejects forged attempts and malformed dimensions, and returns the attempt-scoped usage identity. Single settlement no longer selects ledger replay state before insertion or polls afterward; it targets the reservation/phase constraint and updates reservation consumption only when its authoritative ledger insertion exists. Conflicting uniqueness is classified instead of leaked as a raw database error. Dialect-specific overrun `INSERT OR IGNORE` was replaced with PostgreSQL-compatible conflict handling. The copied zero-release writer was deleted; the sole settlement transaction remains below the focused-module target after aggregate-coverage enforcement, and all release callers delegate to it. Stable retryable, terminal, and operator-action failure classification is SDK-owned and lifecycle-integrated. Explicit overrun approve/reject is team-authorized, idempotent, audited, API/CLI exposed, and service-proven; CAP-131 and CAP-133 are resolved.

The next CAP-129 slice added `POST /v1/provider/assignments/:assignmentId/usage`, the canonical provider-client operation, and runner delivery of every execution-provider usage dimension before terminal settlement. A 142-line typed usage service owns the only `capacity_usage_actuals` insert operation used by both partial reporting and settlement. A reservation-owned `usage_report_token` serializes report transactions against `settlement_token`; same-report replay is idempotent, changed payloads conflict, and reports after settlement fail closed. The clean contract and schema classify every row as `informational`, `incremental`, or `aggregate`. Informational rows must carry zero credits. Terminal aggregate credits must cover all accepted incremental credits; an underreported aggregate is transactionally removed, releases its settlement claim, writes no counter/ledger result, and supports a corrected retry. Workday summaries count every forensic report row but count assignment actuals once and prefer the terminal aggregate for credit/native totals, preventing double-counting. SDK/API/Agent builds pass; SDK provider protocol passes 8/8; Agent assignment runtime passes 12/12; the focused API dimensional-report/settlement lifecycle, corrected-underreport retry, and distinct-key convergence pass. The reconciled local PostgreSQL container then ran the isolated proof in random databases with cleanup: a forced final-operation failure rolled back settlement token, aggregate usage, and ledger writes, and the subsequent late-report-versus-two-settlement race produced one authoritative settlement, one replay, one aggregate usage row, and one consumed reservation. All four PostgreSQL proof cases pass; CAP-128 and CAP-129 are resolved.

The expired-lease slice resolves CAP-130 and CAP-132. Lease acquisition no longer treats an expired `leased` row as a fresh candidate or overwrites its token. A 132-line API recovery service performs a bounded, deterministic scan and classifies each abandoned attempt as safe retry, retry-exhausted terminal failure, recovered completion, or operator-action uncertainty from durable settlement, usage, mode-run, TreeDX, and fallback evidence. It closes orphan mode runs and commits assignment CAS, demand/participation state, and the audit event in one database batch. Retry exhaustion delegates to the sole exactly-once settlement service; if settlement commits and execution stops before the lifecycle batch, the next recovery recognizes that recovery settlement and converges the assignment to failed. The same convergence applies to a committed provider-failure settlement. An injected service test stops after financial commit but before lifecycle transition, verifies the reservation is consumed while the assignment remains leased, then proves expiry recovery reaches one failed terminal result. Polling invokes team/provider-scoped recovery before selection, while operations-runner maintenance invokes bounded global recovery. Safe retry preserves the original reservation; uncertain financial or side-effect evidence remains visibly expired and cannot be requeued until its reservation is financially resolved. Repeated recovery is a no-op. Focused recovery, maintenance, stale-runner, workday requeue, and registration/polling suites pass, and the API build passes.

One typed usage service owns attempt/dimension/idempotency ingestion; one exactly-once settlement transaction owns aggregate usage, ledger, reservation, and counter effects; database uniqueness rather than replay reads selects the winner; failure classification, overrun holds/approval, expired-lease recovery, settlement-before-lifecycle convergence, and stale-runner fencing are durable and service-proven. ### Phases 2-4 and 6

**Implemented:** global Ed25519 provider identities; one revealable/regeneratable team registration key; signed, replay-protected registration requests; approval-only memberships; one-time membership credentials; short-lived access tokens; credential list/rotation/revocation; membership suspension/revocation; membership-scoped availability sessions; manifest-v2 multi-team provider coordination; strict grant/allocation-v2 services; one SDK admission evaluator; transactional reservation/assignment/counter claims; lease-authority validation and state-version compare-and-swap; exactly-once settlement; and reservation/counter recovery on membership invalidation.
**Removed:** the SDK standalone reservation and usage reporters, API standalone usage routes, direct reservation writer, legacy agent provider registration/heartbeat modules, and the duplicate provider-manager/remote-runner/workday-manager stacks.
**Evidence:** focused API governance, grant, allocation, admission, settlement, suspension, lease, and recovery service suites pass; provider runtime multi-team and settlement suites pass; SDK contract suites and package builds pass.
**Historical result:** phases 2, 3, 4, and 6 were once marked complete from partial focused evidence. **Current result:** Phases 0-7 have now closed in canonical order with their required service and concurrency evidence; Phase 8 is active.

### Phases 5, 7-11

**Implemented so far:** `AgentKernel.runAssignment` is the sole kernel entrypoint; plan-only adapter terminology and legacy kernel cycle methods were removed; kernel policy, fallback, run types, runtime helpers, assignment tool catalog, TreeDX handle policy, agent-spec loading, and value utilities are focused modules; runner completion settles before completing and failure settles exactly once; structured artifact manifests fail closed when a completed execution has no durable evidence; starter handler shims were deleted; engineering/research activity profiles were strengthened; research tools are governed and assignment-scoped; team registration/request/membership/credential/grant/allocation/workday CLI actions exist; provider identity/manifest/join/status/leave commands exist; and provider-global weighted multi-team scheduling exists. Fair planning coverage is now persisted in API-owned cycles and entries rather than reconstructed from assignment metadata.

**Implemented since the initial ledger:** the legacy university scenario and duplicate unified-demo capacity flow were deleted instead of migrated; production objective loading and market knowledge promotion no longer read or mutate content directly; arbitrary tool-descriptor metadata is removed before MCP transport; team workday/provider-session/assignment/execution-run operator routes were extracted into a typed route module; and the kernel now resolves core-objective context only from TreeDX evidence. Complete configured activity-profile selection replaced handler-only substitution, workday agent policy is configuration-driven rather than slug-driven, project synthesis is fair, and stale class references are updated through the API.

**Client consolidation:** the SDK provider protocol client owns provider onboarding, signed status, credential exchange, access-token minting, membership leave, identity rotation, approved availability/assignment lifecycle, and settlement over one timeout/error/contract transport. The duplicate Agent governance client and its package artifact were deleted. `MarketClient` is now the sole human/operator transport after the remaining `ControlPlaneClient` workflow consumer, implementation, public exports, and duplicate tests were removed. The runner-token reporter remains separate and narrowly scoped.

**Workday terminalization:** explicit deadlines are preserved and every running verification workday must declare a positive duration or valid deadline; the old inferred one-hour fallback was deleted. A reached deadline immediately pauses run-owned envelopes so no new admission can occur. Valid active leases and their reservations remain intact through the bounded settlement grace; after grace, terminalization exactly-once zero-settles only still-unfinished work, fails unfinished mode runs, closes assignments and every run-owned envelope (including zero-assignment envelopes), persists summary/metrics/actual records, emits terminal evidence, and distinguishes completed from degraded outcomes. Team-owned grants remain active until an explicit operator lifecycle action or declared expiry; workday maintenance never mutates governance policy. The API operations runner invokes maintenance on a bounded interval, so closure no longer depends on a later read request. A reporting activity profile compiles summary and handoff demand through the same durable path; full TreeDX service proof remains required.

**Governed workday scheduling correction:** the API scheduler is now a policy consumer. Before any workday envelope is written it resolves an approved provider membership, every requested project (with no silent subset), one active and effective team allocation covering every project, one unambiguous unexpired planning grant for each project/environment, and an existing TreeDX repository binding. It never creates or supersedes allocation/grant policy and never rewrites project content configuration. CLI `local` resolution uses the unique approved provider with an open local availability session and never forwards the literal selector. Schedule failures persist a failed run and terminalize any partial envelopes. Focused API/CLI tests and the full first-cycle live workday prove the path; CAP-034 is resolved.

**Plan purity:** `trsd capacity workday-run` now returns before class synchronization, TreeDX reconciliation, or workday-run creation when rendering a plan. Live mode alone synchronizes configured profiles and creates bounded control-plane records.

**Superseded historical fair-scheduling conclusion:** earlier focused evidence treated assignment-metadata reconstruction as a strict durable participation barrier. The Phase 5 opening audit disproved that conclusion. The replacement now persists cycle and entry records with explicit exclusion/blockage and does not open a successor while eligible entries remain uncovered. The historical selector evidence is retained only as history; current Phase 5 exit still requires the full concurrent service-path proof.

**Diagnostics extraction:** project capacity pressure/native-budget reporting is now a focused typed API service and `ProjectCapacityDiagnostics` SDK contract exposed by `trsd capacity diagnostics`. Stale provider route projections and the legacy read-only `capacity plan --project` surface were removed.

**Local reset and repository-plane recovery:** the audit proved that the former reset path retained named volumes and preserved stale control-plane rows, while already-running API/operations-runner processes were not restarted. The canonical fix marks selected disposable Compose units for volume/data replacement and maps reset to deterministic managed-process restart. A corrected live reset replaced PostgreSQL and TreeDX volumes, restarted API and operations-runner processes, produced zero teams/providers/memberships/grants/allocations before seed apply, seeded nine project repositories, and then produced an all-NOOP repeat plan. CAP-029 is resolved. The TreeDX repository plane now has content digests, live repository observation, and per-project progress instead of unconditional reseeding; transport timeout/consolidation remains under CAP-030/CAP-031.

**Remaining:** convert the existing duration/budget, repeated-cycle, terminalization, and durable TreeDX summary live evidence into repeatable interruption/replay/concurrency service guarantees; extract the other capacity domains from untyped oversized composition modules; and prove autonomous engineering and cited external research against the integrated starter fixture.

CAP-091 later removed that projection because it reconstructed an incomplete object under the canonical `ProviderAssignment` name and failed strict validation. Current assignment reads use the complete strict row; any later bandwidth optimization must introduce a distinct typed assignment-summary resource rather than another partial assignment shape.
- Retry-free local lifecycle proof on 2026-07-17: one canonical `trsd dev stop` followed by one `trsd dev start --web-runtime local --json` reached `ok: true` without a second start. Reconciler verification reported ready/verified PostgreSQL, Mailpit, TreeDX, capacity-provider manager/runner, API, operations runner, Market web, and all nine exact TreeDX repositories. CAP-049 is resolved.
- Assignment operator parity proof on 2026-07-17: API route/store integration tests, keyset persistence tests, and API/CLI builds pass. After restarting the local API on the changed source, `capacity assignments --assignment <tester-id> --limit 1` returned exactly one matching record and its `{items,page}` metadata; a live opaque cursor then advanced from cycle-two Architect to the distinct Tester record. CAP-050 is resolved without CLI-side truncation or a compatibility response shape.

## Final Readiness Checklist

Local production readiness is complete because:

- one provider securely serves multiple independently governed teams;
- membership alone cannot execute work;
- registration rotation, credentials, access tokens, and revocation work under concurrency;
- provider-global and team allocation limits cannot be oversubscribed;
- stored allocation policy demonstrably governs admission;
- reservations, leases, usage, and settlement are idempotent and transactional;
- workdays perform continuing useful planning and gated acting with fair participation;
- engineering and research starters complete real autonomous workflows;
- all content is TreeDX-backed, linked, traceable, and emitted once;
- every backend action is available through API and CLI, with config where declarative;
- all duplicate, legacy, placeholder, compatibility, direct-mutation, and unused paths are gone;
- capacity/agent code is typed, modular, independently buildable, and service-proven;
- local acceptance passes with cleanup and a separate cleanup observes zero drift;
- documentation matches executable behavior and this ledger has no open local production issue.

Hosted readiness remains separately blocked until CAP-024 is deliberately resumed and hosted acceptance passes through the reviewed reconciliation architecture. That hosted suspension does not authorize a direct deployment bypass and does not block local supervised project development.
